# LANES — the one live dispatch board
Status: LIVE
Reconciled-Against: git log --all + docs/dispatches/ high-water marks, 2026-08-13 (Voice 2 — `8de399e`, `d459c97` on `roadmap`; `2b757b2` on `main`)

**A number is not yours until its row is on this board.** Numbers assigned from chat memory
collide — that is not hypothetical, it happened this morning (see COLLISIONS below). This file
is the only place a dispatch number is issued, and reading it is how a session learns what is
already taken.

Exempt from the Naming Law's "never overwrite" rule, same as `INDEX.md`, `BACKLOG.md`,
`techdebt/LATEST_DEBT.md` and `HIP_HANDOFF.md` — a board whose whole job is to be current
cannot be versioned per update. See the note under NUMBER-CLAIM LAW in `CLAUDE.md`.

---

It reports **WHAT** (pid, elapsed, matched command) and **WHERE** (working directory resolved via `lsof -d cwd`) — that is what turns "something is running" into "something is running IN THIS LANE" — and it **echoes the patterns and ports it scanned even when it finds nothing**, because a silent pass is indistinguishable from a scan that never ran. **⚠ THE DISTINCTION THAT KEEPS THE TOOL USABLE, found while building it on this machine: A RESIDENT SERVICE IS NOT MID-RUN.** `server.demo_dashboard` (pid 1983) held **four ESTABLISHED sockets to 7690** with 44 minutes uptime while listening on 7872. A naive scan called that BUSY — which would have made the demo lane **permanently** blocked and the gate **permanently ignored**, and that is how a gate dies. A process holding a LISTEN socket is a server: its pooled connections report as OCCUPIED and do not refuse. **A battery is not a server** — it opens connections, works, exits — so the blocking signal is a work process or an UNATTRIBUTED client connection. **SCOPING:** the lane gate blocks on work processes whose cwd is inside THIS tree plus non-resident connections on THIS lane's port; a battery in another tree does not block this one unless it is on this lane's graph, which the second term catches. Blocking on machine-wide activity would make the gate noise, and noise is ignored. Bare `--busy` is the machine-wide form — **the check FM 12's precondition should have been able to call.** **FAIL-CLOSED: `ps`/`lsof` failure is exit 8, SCAN UNAVAILABLE, never a silent green** — the same rule `push_docs.sh` now follows for its pattern, proven in the twin with a broken `PATH`. **detection goes green again once that process exits, so it is live rather than sticky**; a live connection is refused and its owner named; the LISTENER in the same test is classified resident and does NOT refuse; and a broken scan is exit 8. **The twin creates its own subject and scans its own port** — a twin that scanned the real ports would be measuring the weather, passing on a quiet machine and failing during someone else's battery, proving nothing about the tool. **LIVE RESULT ON THE FIRST REAL RUN:** it found the four resident dashboard sockets (correctly not blocking) **and three live processes in `~/hip-vo` two seconds old belonging to another session** — the FM 12 case exactly: real activity in another lane that no lock records and no dispatch would have seen without asking `ps`. **HONEST NOTE, the FM 9 shape again:** the new twin's first run went RED on "names WHERE" and **the tool was not the problem** — the twin spawned its subject without a working directory of its own, so there was nothing distinctive to find; fixed in the twin and recorded, because a twin wrong in the green direction would have passed a broken tool just as easily. **FILED, NOT BLOCKING (2): (FM14-1)** a pattern list is a name list and is never complete; So the acceptance itself had to change shape, and **the rule is now written into the REQ (E1-E7): a twin that injects the condition it is testing does not satisfy it.** **(1) STORE-DOWN, PROVEN ON THE DEFAULT PROBE.** The default probe was `read_user_facts`, which is **CONTRACTUALLY NON-RAISING** — its own docstring promises `[]` on any failure *including "Neo4j unreachable"*, precisely so retrieval can never break a turn (TD-011/TD-023) — so the kernel's `except → False` branch was **dead code**. Replaced with `_driver_for_probe()` + `verify_connectivity()`, which RAISES: a **throwaway** driver, never cached (caching one built while the store was down would poison every later call; reusing a live one would hide a store that has since gone away), 3s connect timeout. **UNREACHABLE is now distinguished from EMPTY — a store that answers "no facts" is up, a store that cannot be dialled is down.** Measured with the graph genuinely down: `store_reachable()` **False** where NC 10 measured **True**, and the household turn is **REFUSED_STORE_DOWN with 0 model calls** where NC 10 measured ANSWERED, model called, reply *"Wednesdays"*. **(2) WEB SEARCH, PROVEN ON THE REAL GATEWAY.** The strip removes facts from CONTEXT and **never rewrites the query string**, so `sent == query` always and safe-iff-unchanged was vacuous. The kernel now derives the outbound query's safety **itself**, in three named classes ordered by what they cost: an **enrolled member's name** (the registry is the authority on who this household is), a **street-address SHAPE** — matched as a shape, **not against a stored value, because a check that had to read the graph would be unavailable exactly when the store is down** — and **household possessives**. It returns the REFERENT rather than a bool so a refusal can say which class fired (**a refusal that cannot explain itself gets switched off**), and it **fails closed when the registry is unreadable**: if we cannot enumerate who lives here, we cannot say a query names nobody. **On the real path, real registry, sockets made impossible: NC 9's own example `"when is bill's cardiology appointment"` → REFUSED (`member-name:Bill`); `"...at [REDACTED-HOME-ADDRESS]"` → REFUSED (`address-shape`); `"what is an R-1-18 zone setback requirement"` → PERMITTED unchanged** — where NC 10 measured all three PERMITTED and unchanged. `query_is_safe_to_send` keeps its `stripped_query` parameter **and ignores it**, deliberately: the argument that used to decide is now **visibly inert rather than quietly deleted**. **(3) `model_called` is OBSERVED, not asserted** — a **contextvar** counter (not a global: turns are concurrent on the voice path and a module counter would attribute one turn's call to another's result), incremented **AT** the line that opens the completion at both answering sites in `_governed_turn`. NC 10's own method: impl calling no model → `False, observed: 0`; impl calling one → `True, 1`. This matters because `process_governed_turn` returns structural replies with no model call on several paths — INJ-7, the park gate, the confirmation gate — so the field whose whole purpose is to be trustworthy was lying on every one. **12 ACCEPTANCE TWINS, ALL ON THE DEFAULT PATH — no injected probe, no hand-built payloads, the REAL classifier INITIALISED rather than monkeypatched**, which removes the limit NC 10 reported honestly (it could not exercise "a public turn still answers" because an uninitialised classifier makes everything household-dependent). **52 tests across NC 8 / NC 9 / NC 11. ### `Voice 39` — AMBIGUOUS CITATION, flagged 2026-08-14, NOT a board collision

**The board was not double-claimed and the law worked.** But a bare `Voice 39` citation now resolves
to two different pieces of work, and this note is how a reader tells them apart:

| where | what it means |
|---|---|
| commit subject of **`3505443`** | a session on `~/hip-vo` @ `main` **self-labelling** as "Voice 39" while claiming **HA-74**. It changed the `main` Advisor row only and **never touched the Voice row** |
| **this board row**, claimed 2026-08-14 | the actual `Voice 39` — MOSHI LAB drift measurement, `ML-02` |

**Why this is not a collision:** NUMBER-CLAIM LAW says *the board issues the number; the chat does
not*. The Voice row read `Voice 38` when this dispatch started, so `Voice 39` was free and was
claimed here. `3505443` claimed `HA-74`, which is what its board row says and what it actually took.

**Why it is still worth recording:** `git log --grep="Voice 39"` finds that commit and not this
work, which is the same "findable from its number alone" defect the dispatch-ID naming rule exists
to fix. **The gap the board does not close: a session's SELF-LABEL in a commit subject is
unpoliced.** Nothing stops a chat writing any `Voice N` it likes into a subject line while claiming
a different number on the board — and a reader greps subjects, not rows. Recorded, not fixed; a fix
would be a rule about commit subjects, which is Bill's to make.

### HA-50, HA-51 — NOT collisions, but the same root cause

Both numbers appear on both branches. They are **one dispatch each, whose parts landed on
different branches**: HA-50 Part A on `roadmap` (`6884aca`), Part B on `main` (`c05b273`);
HA-51 Parts A+B on `main` (`a362d5b`, `8fd0b52`), Part C on `roadmap` (`f1c687f`). Recorded here
because it is the same underlying fact as HA-66 — **the HA series has been minted from two
diverged branches since 2026-08-12** — and because a reader who greps `HA-50` finds two commits
and needs to know they are one dispatch, not two.

### `Voice N` — RESOLVED by Bill's ruling, 2026-08-13

This chat's lane is named `Voice` and started at `Voice 1`. A `Voice N` series already ran on
`~/hip-vo` @ `voice-port` and reached **Voice 37**, so a bare `Voice 1` or `Voice 2` citation
was ambiguous between the two — the same defect as HA-66.

**Bill's ruling: this chat CONTINUES the existing series. Its next dispatch is `Voice 38`.**
**Voice 1 and Voice 2 are left as issued and are NOT renumbered** — the same reasoning that
keeps `D-R-nnn` and `TD-R-nnn` intact under CLAUDE.md item 10: renumbering rewrites identifiers
that documents, commits and registers already cite. So `Voice 1` and `Voice 2` each name two
different dispatches, eleven days apart, and this note is how a reader tells them apart:

| citation | 2026-08-01/02, `voice-port` | 2026-08-13, this chat |
|---|---|---|
| `Voice 1` | (early series, pre-`Voice 22` commits not on any pushed branch) | M0 moshi research spike — STOPPED at the isolation gate |
| `Voice 2` | ″ | this board + NUMBER-CLAIM/RECAP-IDENTITY laws + the HA-69 renumber |

**From Voice 38 forward the ambiguity ends** — every number is above the old series' high-water.

## NOTES

- **2026-08-14 (NC 19 cont., BILL'S RULING 1) — ⭐ THE 7690 HOLD IS RELEASED. ORDINARY DEMO OPERATION, INCLUDING `demo_reset`, IS PERMITTED AGAIN.** The hold was placed to protect 7690's *"stranded envelopes"* — **that basis is DISPROVEN.** **FM 26's forensics found the FM 24 alarm was a FALSE ALARM: the data is intact, 13/13.** There is nothing stranded to protect, so the restriction the hold imposed has no remaining subject. **FM 27's repair stands on its own** and is **not** the basis of this release — it was independently sound, and the release rests on FM 26's measurement, not on FM 27 having patched anything. **The re-seed/re-key prohibition recorded against 7690 above (Live lanes row) is lifted by this ruling**; it is left in place as history rather than deleted, per the standing rule that a superseded instruction is annotated, never silently removed. **Released by Bill, recorded by NC 19 — a session did not release its own hold.**

- **2026-08-14 (VD-65) — working-copy drift found and restored; no committed content changed.** `~/hip-cutover-demo`'s copy of the VD-63 freeze record had been overwritten by a stray ~20-line paste of FM 11's rotation report (2080 bytes shadowing 10050). HEAD was never affected; `git checkout --` on that one file restored it byte-identically (sha `5966c4d345f8bc88` before and after). Stray content preserved at `/tmp/VD65_stray_fm11_paste_from_vd63_freeze_doc__20260814.md`. **This section did not exist until VD-65; it was created because the dispatch named it.**

## LIMITS — read before trusting this board

1. **`main` cannot see this file.** `roadmap` and `main` diverged at `688386f` (2026-07-18) and
   are 430 / 107 commits apart. This board lives on `roadmap`. Until the branches reconcile, a
   dispatch running in `~/hip-vo` claims its number against a board it does not have — which is
   the HA-66 failure mode still live. **This is the one thing that would make the board actually
   work, and it is not fixed by this dispatch.**
2. **The demo lane's IN FLIGHT is unknown from here.** Read from commit subjects on
   `demo-cutover-build`, not from that lane's own report.
3. **High-water marks are read from commit subjects and filenames.** A number claimed in a chat
   and not yet committed is invisible to this method — which is precisely why NUMBER-CLAIM LAW
   makes the board row, not the chat, the thing that issues a number.
4. **The HA-69 renumber left ~12 `HA-66` attribution comments behind on `main`**, across nine
   files: `.gitignore`, `docs/techdebt/DEBT_REGISTER__v20260813_0914.md`, the lane-safety clause
   header in `REQ_SUITE_EXPECTATION_RECONCILIATION__…v20260813.md`, `harness/lane_ownership.py`,
   `harness/speaker_id.py`, `scripts/gate_check.sh`, `scripts/demo_reset.py`,
   `scripts/demo_seed.py`, `eval/test_lane_ownership.py`. Bill's ruling scoped the renumber to
   filename, header, INDEX row and pointer line; six of the nine are code files, and HA-68 was
   live on that branch at the time. **So a grep for `HA-69` does not find the code it built.**
   Left for Bill to rule on — a sweep is a code-file change and needs its own authorization.
5. **`main` runs an older `CLAUDE.md`.** No STANDARD PREAMBLE, so no machine gate, no lock rule,
   no lane-prefix table (item 10), no never-overwrite list. That is not incidental to the HA-66
   collision — **a session on `main` was never told that per-lane prefixes exist**, which is why
   it minted an HA number the way it did. Reconciling the two CLAUDE.md files is the other half
   of the fix and is not attempted here.
